The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of EMPO on mathematical reasoning and free-form commonsense reasoning tasks, showing competitive performance compared to supervised methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the **fully unsupervised approach** to LLM reasoning incentivization.  Prior works always involved *some* form of supervision, be it human-labeled traces, golden answers, pre-trained reward models, self-consistency for pseudo-labeling, test-case evaluation, or initialization of RL from instruction-tuned frontier LLMs. EMPO's reliance solely on minimizing semantic entropy on unlabeled data is a significant departure and a novel exploration. The idea of using semantic entropy as a proxy for quality in the absence of explicit rewards is also relatively novel in the context of RL for LLMs.
*   **Significance:** If EMPO proves robust and generalizable, it has the potential to be quite significant. The dependence on costly and scarce human supervision is a major bottleneck in scaling LLM reasoning abilities. EMPO addresses this bottleneck directly. The empirical results, showing performance competitive with SFT and supervised RL, are encouraging. The results on TruthfulQA are particularly interesting given the challenge of this benchmark.  The ability to improve truthfulness *without* any external "truth" signal is valuable.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addressing a key limitation:** Directly tackles the scalability issue of current LLM reasoning enhancement techniques.
    *   **Principled approach:** EMPO is grounded in the well-established concept of entropy minimization and its connection to model uncertainty.  The paper provides some theoretical justification for using semantic entropy.
    *   **Empirical validation:** The experiments are comprehensive, covering both mathematical and free-form reasoning tasks, with favorable results relative to baselines. The paper also has an ablation component highlighting the importance of the constraint.
    *   **Clear and well-written:** The paper is easy to follow and understand.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependence on Semantic Clustering:** The reliance on semantic clustering introduces its own complexities and potential for bias. The paper states BERT-like model is used for determining if two outputs are of the same meaning. The effectiveness of EMPO is inherently tied to the accuracy and quality of this clustering process. The paper does not delve deeply into the sensitivity of EMPO to the choice of the semantic clustering method or its parameters.
    *   **Reward Hacking Mitigation:** While the paper mentions and attempts to mitigate reward hacking with entropy thresholding, it acknowledges that this is a simple strategy, and potentially other modes of reward hacking could appear.  A more rigorous analysis of the potential vulnerabilities of the unsupervised objective is needed.
    *   **Generalizability:** While the results are strong on the tasks considered, it's unclear how well EMPO would generalize to other, more complex reasoning tasks or different LLM architectures.  Also, are the mathematical problems simple enough that simply forcing consistency would make the model perform better?
    *   **Unclear Training Details:** While the paper states "the reasoning prompt template we used is as follow" it doesn't describe the actual prompt it uses when training.
    *   **Semantic Clustering Method:**  The paper states "we leverage DeBERTa-v3-large (a bert-like language model with 304M parameters) to determine whether two outputs are of the same meaning or not [18, 19]. Specifically, if the final predictions (i.e., the contents within "\boxed{}") of two model outputs are bidirectionally implicating, then we merge them into one semantic cluster ignoring their reasoning traces." This is still a form of supervision.

*   **Potential Influence:** EMPO, if further developed and validated, could significantly impact how we train reasoning LLMs, making them more accessible and scalable. It could also spur research into other unsupervised objectives for language model training.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the novel fully unsupervised approach and the encouraging initial results, but acknowledging the limitations regarding semantic clustering sensitivity, reward hacking, generalizability, and some unclarities, I'm giving the paper a score of **7**. The idea is promising and addresses a critical need, but further investigation is required to fully realize its potential and address the identified weaknesses.
Score: 7
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Parasite: A Steganography-based Backdoor Attack Framework for Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05815v1)
- **Authors**: Jiahao Chen, Yu Pan, Yi Du, Chunkai Wu, Lin Wang
- **Abstract**: Recently, the diffusion model has gained significant attention as one of the most successful image generation models, which can generate high-quality images by iteratively sampling noise. However, recent studies have shown that diffusion models are vulnerable to backdoor attacks, allowing attackers to enter input data containing triggers to activate the backdoor and generate their desired output. Existing backdoor attack methods primarily focused on target noise-to-image and text-to-image tasks, with limited work on backdoor attacks in image-to-image tasks. Furthermore, traditional backdoor attacks often rely on a single, conspicuous trigger to generate a fixed target image, lacking concealability and flexibility. To address these limitations, we propose a novel backdoor attack method called "Parasite" for image-to-image tasks in diffusion models, which not only is the first to leverage steganography for triggers hiding, but also allows attackers to embed the target content as a backdoor trigger to achieve a more flexible attack. "Parasite" as a novel attack method effectively bypasses existing detection frameworks to execute backdoor attacks. In our experiments, "Parasite" achieved a 0 percent backdoor detection rate against the mainstream defense frameworks. In addition, in the ablation study, we discuss the influence of different hiding coefficients on the attack results. You can find our code at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/Parasite-1715/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Parasite," a novel backdoor attack framework targeting image-to-image tasks in diffusion models. Unlike existing backdoor attack methods that typically use conspicuous triggers and focus on noise-to-image or text-to-image tasks, Parasite leverages steganography to hide backdoor triggers within images, making them nearly imperceptible. This approach enhances the concealability of the attack and allows attackers to embed custom target content as the backdoor trigger, offering more flexibility in attack outcomes. The authors demonstrate that Parasite can effectively bypass existing backdoor detection frameworks, achieving a 0% detection rate in experiments against mainstream defense methods. They also investigate the impact of varying steganography hiding coefficients and line complexities on the attack's success.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the integration of steganography for hiding backdoor triggers in image-to-image diffusion models. This is a significant departure from traditional backdoor attacks that rely on more obvious visual or textual triggers. The ability to customize the attack outcome by embedding target content is also a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The research highlights a new vulnerability in diffusion models, demonstrating that attackers can circumvent existing defenses by utilizing sophisticated steganographic techniques. This raises serious security concerns, especially considering the growing adoption of diffusion models in various applications. The practical implications of undetectable backdoor attacks with customizable outcomes are substantial, emphasizing the need for more robust defense mechanisms.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear and well-defined problem statement.
    *   Novel use of steganography to enhance backdoor attack concealability.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation, including comparisons with existing defense frameworks.
    *   Analysis of the impact of different parameters on attack success.
    *   The practical implementation is relatively straightforward.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper shows that Parasite can bypass existing defenses, it does not propose any new defense mechanisms to counter such attacks.
    *   The experimental evaluation primarily focuses on ASR and FID scores, which may not fully capture the nuances of backdoor attacks in image-to-image tasks. More sophisticated evaluation metrics might be necessary.
    *   The paper should offer some discussion and possible mitigation solutions to solve the vulnerabilities it outlines.
    *   The success of the steganographic approach might depend on the specific characteristics of the images being used.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a considerable impact on the field of generative model security. By highlighting a new attack vector, it encourages researchers to develop more sophisticated detection and defense strategies that consider steganographic techniques. It will also prompt further investigation into the robustness of diffusion models against subtle, hidden attacks.

*   **Justification for Score:**  Although the paper presents a well-executed demonstration of a new attack vector, it does not provide comprehensive defense mechanisms, nor does it delve into all the potential factors that could affect the results. The findings are significant. Given the significant impact and novelty, but also with limitations mentioned above.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Leveraging Robust Optimization for LLM Alignment under Distribution Shifts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05831v1)
- **Authors**: Mingye Zhu, Yi Liu, Junbo Guo, Quan Wang, Yongdong Zhang, Zhendong Mao
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) increasingly rely on preference alignment methods to steer outputs toward human values, yet these methods are often constrained by the scarcity of high-quality human-annotated data. To tackle this, recent approaches have turned to synthetic data generated by LLMs as a scalable alternative. However, synthetic data can introduce distribution shifts, compromising the nuanced human preferences that are essential for desirable outputs. In this paper, we propose a novel distribution-aware optimization framework that improves preference alignment in the presence of such shifts. Our approach first estimates the likelihood ratios between the target and training distributions leveraging a learned classifier, then it minimizes the worst-case loss over data regions that reflect the target human-preferred distribution. By explicitly prioritizing the target distribution during optimization, our method mitigates the adverse effects of distributional variation and enhances the generation of responses that faithfully reflect human values.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Leveraging Robust Optimization for LLM Alignment under Distribution Shifts":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of aligning Large Language Models (LLMs) with human values when using synthetic data for training. Synthetic data, while scalable, often introduces distribution shifts that can compromise the nuances of human preferences. To mitigate this, the authors propose Distribution-aware optimization for Robust Alignment (DoRA). DoRA first estimates the likelihood ratios between the target (human-preferred) and training distributions using a learned classifier. Then, it optimizes the model by minimizing the worst-case loss over data regions that represent the target distribution. This explicitly prioritizes the target distribution during optimization, mitigating the adverse effects of distributional variation and enhancing the generation of responses that more faithfully reflect human values.  The authors validate their approach through experiments on multiple models and tasks, comparing DoRA to state-of-the-art baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of addressing distribution shifts in LLM alignment through robust optimization techniques is reasonably novel. While robust optimization and likelihood ratio estimation are established techniques, their specific application within this context to handle synthetic data biases appears to be a valuable contribution.
* **Significance:**  The paper addresses a practically significant problem.  The reliance on synthetic data for LLM alignment is increasing, and the associated biases are a major hurdle. A robust method for handling these biases has the potential to significantly improve the quality and reliability of LLM outputs. The demonstrated effectiveness of DoRA across various tasks is a strong point.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of distribution shifts introduced by synthetic data.
    * **Well-Defined Methodology:** The proposed DoRA framework is clearly explained, with a principled approach to estimating likelihood ratios and minimizing worst-case loss.
    * **Empirical Validation:** The paper includes thorough experimental validation on multiple models (Pythia-6.9B, Mistral-7B-v0.1) and datasets (HH-RLHF, Summarization, UltraFeedback).
    * **Comparison with Baselines:** DoRA is compared against strong baselines like DPO, RRHF and LIRE, showing consistent improvements.
    * **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies help to understand the contribution of each component of the framework. In particular effectiveness of the bias-mitigation mechanism is well demonstrated.
    * **Analysis and Visualization:** The paper presents results with supporting visuals. Density distributions show how DoRA is effectively balancing with the desired outcomes.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on a Classifier:** The approach relies on a trained classifier to estimate likelihood ratios. The performance of DoRA is directly tied to the accuracy of this classifier. The choice of BERT base as the classifier model seems arbitrary and its potential impact on the performance has not been discussed. A deeper analysis of classifier performance and sensitivity would strengthen the paper.
    * **Limited Exploration of Divergence Measures:** While the paper motivates the use of KL divergence for its tractability, it acknowledges that other divergence measures (e.g., Wasserstein distance) could be considered. Exploring these alternatives, even briefly, would broaden the scope of the work.
    * **Lack of a deeper theoretical analysis:** While a high-level convergence analysis is provided, a more rigorous theoretical analysis of the bounds on the alignment error would be beneficial.
    * **Limited Novelty in Methodology:** Robust optimization and likelihood ratio estimation are not new techniques. The contribution lies in their application to the specific problem of LLM alignment with synthetic data.
* **Impact:** If the DoRA framework proves to be widely applicable and effective, it could significantly influence how LLMs are trained and aligned, particularly as the reliance on synthetic data increases. The approach promotes better and more reliable alignment with human preferences, even when facing data biases.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a well-motivated, practically relevant, and empirically supported solution to the significant challenge of distribution shifts in LLM alignment using synthetic data. The novelty lies primarily in the application of robust optimization and likelihood ratio estimation in this specific context. The experimental results clearly demonstrate the advantages of DoRA over existing baselines. While there are some weaknesses, namely the dependence on a classifier and the reliance on a single divergence measure, the strengths of the paper outweigh its limitations. Given the significance of the problem and the solid empirical evidence supporting the effectiveness of DoRA, it represents a substantial contribution to the field.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Mind the Trojan Horse: Image Prompt Adapter Enabling Scalable and Deceptive Jailbreaking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05838v1)
- **Authors**: Junxi Chen, Junhao Dong, Xiaohua Xie
- **Abstract**: Recently, the Image Prompt Adapter (IP-Adapter) has been increasingly integrated into text-to-image diffusion models (T2I-DMs) to improve controllability. However, in this paper, we reveal that T2I-DMs equipped with the IP-Adapter (T2I-IP-DMs) enable a new jailbreak attack named the hijacking attack. We demonstrate that, by uploading imperceptible image-space adversarial examples (AEs), the adversary can hijack massive benign users to jailbreak an Image Generation Service (IGS) driven by T2I-IP-DMs and mislead the public to discredit the service provider. Worse still, the IP-Adapter's dependency on open-source image encoders reduces the knowledge required to craft AEs. Extensive experiments verify the technical feasibility of the hijacking attack. In light of the revealed threat, we investigate several existing defenses and explore combining the IP-Adapter with adversarially trained models to overcome existing defenses' limitations. Our code is available at https://github.com/fhdnskfbeuv/attackIPA.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Mind the Trojan Horse: Image Prompt Adapter Enabling Scalable and Deceptive Jailbreaking":

**Summary:**

This paper introduces a novel jailbreak attack called the "hijacking attack" targeting text-to-image diffusion models (T2I-DMs) that incorporate Image Prompt Adapters (IP-Adapters). The attack leverages the IP-Adapter to jailbreak diffusion models by exploiting adversarial examples (AEs) in the image space. Rather than directly querying the image generation service (IGS) with adversarial prompts, the attacker uploads carefully crafted, visually imperceptible AEs to web platforms. Benign users unintentionally download these AEs and use them as image prompts, unknowingly triggering the generation of NSFW content. Because the adversarial examples are visually benign, users may blame the IGS provider for being biased instead of suspecting the prompt. The attack has better scalability and deceptiveness than direct text-based jailbreaking attacks. The authors propose a method called Attacking Encoder Only (AEO) to craft these AEs, aligning them with NSFW prompts in the encoder feature space. The study demonstrates the feasibility of this attack across several tasks (text-to-image, image inpainting, virtual try-on) and models, and then evaluates existing defenses, showing their limitations. The paper explores using adversarial training with FARE as a mitigation strategy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The concept of using image-space adversarial examples to hijack benign user queries and indirectly jailbreak T2I-DMs is a novel contribution. While text-based jailbreaking has been widely studied, this approach brings a new perspective by exploiting the IP-Adapter mechanism.  The observation that the reliance of IP-Adapters on image encoders opens up a new attack vector is also noteworthy.  The proposed AEO method is a relatively simple yet effective way to craft these AEs.

**Significance:**  The paper highlights a significant security vulnerability in widely used T2I-DMs, particularly those with IP-Adapters. The hijacking attack presents a more realistic threat model than direct adversarial prompting, as it scales the attack via unaware users and makes it more difficult to detect. The potential for damage to IGS providers' reputation is also a critical consideration.  The study's findings are relevant and potentially have significant impact in practical situations. The defense discussion is also valuable, though not groundbreaking. It raises important questions about the limitations of existing mitigation techniques and the need for new approaches.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly defines the problem and its potential consequences.
*   **Realistic Threat Model:** The hijacking attack reflects a more likely real-world scenario.
*   **Extensive Evaluation:** The experiments cover a diverse set of models and tasks.
*   **Feasibility Demonstrated:** Experiments on real-world IGSs show the practical threat.
*   **Simple yet Effective Attack (AEO):**  Easy to implement attack vector.
*   **Solid code release.**

**Weaknesses:**

*   **AEO's simplicity:** While a strength, AEO is also a weakness. Is it possible to construct even *less* perceptible attacks?
*   **Limited Defense Exploration:** The defense analysis, while valuable, could be more in-depth. While adversarial training is a reasonable starting point, alternative defense strategies (e.g., input sanitization, more robust anomaly detection) are not fully explored. Is the trade-off between robustness and quality explored?
*   **Limited Control over AEO:** In section F.1, can AEO control *what* content is inserted?
*   **Assumptions:** The assumption that the adversary only has access to *open-source* image encoders in Appendix I seems strange. What are the security implications for services with proprietary image encoders?

**Potential Influence:**  This paper is likely to influence future research on T2I-DM security, particularly regarding indirect or "Trojan horse" style attacks. It motivates further investigation into the vulnerabilities introduced by conditioning mechanisms like IP-Adapters and the development of defenses that can detect or mitigate image-based adversarial prompts. It will also raise awareness among IGS providers about the need to protect against this type of attack.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper is a valuable contribution to the field of T2I-DM security due to its novelty in the attack and real-world practical results. A score of 7 reflects this. It is not a perfect paper due to limitations in the defense exploration and potential over simplicity.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### PathGPT: Leveraging Large Language Models for Personalized Route Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05846v1)
- **Authors**: Steeve Cuthbert Marcelyn, Yucen Gao, Yuzhe Zhang, Xiaofeng Gao, Guihai Chen
- **Abstract**: The proliferation of GPS enabled devices has led to the accumulation of a substantial corpus of historical trajectory data. By leveraging these data for training machine learning models,researchers have devised novel data-driven methodologies that address the personalized route recommendation (PRR) problem. In contrast to conventional algorithms such as Dijkstra shortest path algorithm,these novel algorithms possess the capacity to discern and learn patterns within the data,thereby facilitating the generation of more personalized paths. However,once these models have been trained,their application is constrained to the generation of routes that align with their training patterns. This limitation renders them less adaptable to novel scenarios and the deployment of multiple machine learning models might be necessary to address new possible scenarios,which can be costly as each model must be trained separately. Inspired by recent advances in the field of Large Language Models (LLMs),we leveraged their natural language understanding capabilities to develop a unified model to solve the PRR problem while being seamlessly adaptable to new scenarios without additional training. To accomplish this,we combined the extensive knowledge LLMs acquired during training with further access to external hand-crafted context information,similar to RAG (Retrieved Augmented Generation) systems,to enhance their ability to generate paths according to user-defined requirements. Extensive experiments on different datasets show a considerable uplift in LLM performance on the PRR problem.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "PathGPT: Leveraging Large Language Models for Personalized Route Generation," based on the provided text.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PathGPT, a framework that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) for personalized route recommendation (PRR).  Instead of relying on traditional pathfinding algorithms or training separate machine learning models for each specific user requirement, PathGPT utilizes LLMs' natural language understanding capabilities. It combines LLMs' inherent knowledge with external, hand-crafted contextual information derived from historical trajectory data, similar to a Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) system. The historical path data, originally in the form of edge IDs, is converted into natural language descriptions. During inference, the framework retrieves similar historical paths based on the user query (source, destination, and constraints) and uses these as context to prompt the LLM to generate the route. The authors claim PathGPT offers a unified and adaptable solution to the PRR problem, capable of handling various user-defined requirements without retraining and demonstrating improvements over baseline methods on different datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Incremental, not revolutionary:** The core idea of using LLMs for path generation isn't entirely novel.  The novelty lies in the specific *implementation* and the application to the PRR problem. Using LLMs for reasoning tasks is not new.
*   **RAG adaptation:** Combining LLMs with RAG is a well-established technique in NLP. The adaptation to incorporate historical path data as context is a reasonable extension.
*   **Data transformation:** The conversion of raw edge IDs into natural language descriptions is clever but might be a simple engineering solution rather than a fundamentally novel contribution.
*   **Unified model:** The idea of a "unified model" is valuable, but the experiments would need to strongly demonstrate this claim against fine-tuned models for each requirement. This demonstration is not clear within the context.

**Significance:**

*   **Potential impact:** The paper addresses a practical problem (PRR) with a potentially impactful solution. A unified model capable of handling various constraints without retraining could be valuable.
*   **Efficiency:** If PathGPT truly outperforms traditional methods and specialized ML models in terms of *both* accuracy and efficiency (training time, inference time, resource usage), then it could be significant. This needs strong experimental validation, which is not clear within the context.
*   **Generalizability:** The generalizability of PathGPT beyond the tested datasets is a concern. LLMs' performance can vary significantly based on the availability and quality of training data for specific regions.
*   **Black box challenge:** PathGPT has the general challenge of leveraging LLMs, where it is difficult to fully understand their generation process. This could be mitigated by more interpretable path generation, but such mechanisms are not described.
*   **Lack of Comparative Assessment**: The paper provides metrics for precision and recall of generated paths, comparing the LLM to the ground truth. A comparative assessment on the generated routes in terms of distance and/or travel time with the ones generated via standard algorithms such as Djikstra is absent, thus failing to prove the overall advantage of using PathGPT.

**Strengths:**

*   The paper addresses a relevant problem.
*   It proposes a practical framework (PathGPT) for PRR.
*   The use of RAG provides a reasonable way to inject external knowledge into the LLM.
*   The initial experimental results show improvements over baselines, although the magnitude of improvement and the datasets used might limit the wider applicability.

**Weaknesses:**

*   Lack of substantial novelty (adapting existing techniques).
*   The reliance on LLMs introduces potential issues of hallucination and scalability.
*   Limited evidence of real-world practicality.
*   The improvements in precision and recall could potentially be translated into longer distances and/or travel times, which make the approach unfeasible.
*   The analysis of the generated paths is too simplistic in terms of looking at the metrics (Precision and Recall). A comparison of the real-world path costs with the ones generated by traditionnal algorithms is lacking, thus weakening the overall advantage of PathGPT.
*   Lack of detail on the cost and scalability of the RAG component.
*   Limited analysis of why the LLM improvements occur.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents an interesting adaptation of existing techniques (LLMs and RAG) to a practical problem (PRR). However, the novelty is incremental rather than revolutionary, and the significance is tempered by concerns about generalizability, computational cost, and reliance on potentially unpredictable LLM behavior. Furthermore, the lack of a more thorough comparion makes the advantages difficult to quantify. The experimental results show promising improvements over baselines, but the limited analysis weakens the potential impact of the work.

Therefore, I would assign a score of:

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 6/10

### On the Importance of Conditioning for Privacy-Preserving Data Augmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05849v1)
- **Authors**: Julian Lorenz, Katja Ludwig, Valentin Haug, Rainer Lienhart
- **Abstract**: Latent diffusion models can be used as a powerful augmentation method to artificially extend datasets for enhanced training. To the human eye, these augmented images look very different to the originals. Previous work has suggested to use this data augmentation technique for data anonymization. However, we show that latent diffusion models that are conditioned on features like depth maps or edges to guide the diffusion process are not suitable as a privacy preserving method. We use a contrastive learning approach to train a model that can correctly identify people out of a pool of candidates. Moreover, we demonstrate that anonymization using conditioned diffusion models is susceptible to black box attacks. We attribute the success of the described methods to the conditioning of the latent diffusion model in the anonymization process. The diffusion model is instructed to produce similar edges for the anonymized images. Hence, a model can learn to recognize these patterns for identification.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "On the Importance of Conditioning for Privacy-Preserving Data Augmentation":

**Summary:**

This paper investigates the privacy risks associated with using conditioned latent diffusion models for data augmentation, particularly in the context of anonymizing facial images. The authors demonstrate that even though augmented images generated by these models appear visually different from the originals, the conditioning process (e.g., preserving edges or depth information) inadvertently leaks information that can be exploited to re-identify individuals. They train a contrastive learning model that can successfully identify people in datasets anonymized using instance-level augmentation, even in black-box scenarios where the attacker only has access to edge images derived from the anonymized data. The study highlights the vulnerability of these augmentation techniques for data anonymization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   *Clear Problem Statement:* The paper tackles a relevant and timely problem: the security of privacy-preserving data augmentation techniques, especially given their increasing use in machine learning.
    *   *Well-Defined Methodology:* The authors use a contrastive learning approach with SimCLR which is clearly explained and easy to understand. They also evaluate different edge detection method for their black box attack.
    *   *Comprehensive Experiments:* The experiments are relatively thorough, exploring different conditioning factors (depth, edges, segmentation masks), evaluation protocols (full reference, single reference, few reference), and ablation studies. They also experiment with different backbones and hyperparameter optimization for the contrastive learning model.
    *   *Practical Implications:* The findings have practical implications for those developing and deploying privacy-preserving data augmentation techniques. The paper cautions against relying solely on visual dissimilarity as a measure of privacy and highlights the importance of considering feature-level information leakage.
    *   *Black-Box Attack:* The black-box attack showing that individuals can still be identified based on just the *edges* of anonymized images is a compelling and important result.
    *   *Reproducibility:* The authors state they will release code, weights, and the generated datasets, increasing the reproducibility of their work.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   *Limited Scope:* The evaluation focuses primarily on facial images and the CelebA dataset. While this is a common benchmark, the findings might not generalize to other types of data or augmentation techniques.
    *   *SimCLR Choice:* While the authors state their use of SimCLR is intentional due to their interest in showing simple models suffice, it could be argued more recent/novel contrastive methods could bolster their claims.
    *   *Potential for Overstatement:* While the paper demonstrates a vulnerability, the re-identification rates, especially in the more challenging scenarios (single reference, black-box attack with Canny edge detection), are not exceptionally high. The paper could benefit from a more nuanced discussion of the attack's limitations.
    *   *Mitigation Strategies:* The paper focuses primarily on demonstrating the vulnerability of existing techniques and does not explore or propose specific mitigation strategies to address the identified information leakage.

*   **Novelty:**

    *   The main novelty lies in demonstrating the vulnerability of conditioned latent diffusion models for anonymization, where previous work might have assumed privacy due to visual differences.
    *   The black-box attack using edge images as the sole input for re-identification is also a novel contribution.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper is significant because it raises important questions about the security of privacy-preserving data augmentation and challenges the assumption that visually plausible augmentations necessarily guarantee anonymity.
    *   The findings are relevant to researchers and practitioners working in data privacy, machine learning, and computer vision.
    *   The work calls for the development of more robust privacy metrics and mitigation techniques for data augmentation methods.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper has the potential to influence future research in privacy-preserving data augmentation by shifting the focus from visual dissimilarity to feature-level information leakage.
    *   It could also inform the development of more robust privacy metrics and evaluation methodologies.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7/10** to this paper.

*   The paper addresses an important problem with clear methodology and experimental results that show flaws in prior, less-robustly validated methods for data anonymization. The experiments are well-designed and support the conclusions.
*   The paper highlights limitations in modern augmented training techniques, which will guide further work in the field. The black-box attack based on edges is a particularly compelling result.
*   However, the evaluation is limited in scope (facial images), and the paper does not offer concrete mitigation strategies. While it states that the model only needs simple methods, it could have been improved with a newer contrastive learning technique. This reduces the potential impact compared to work that proposes a specific solution.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Physics-aware generative models for turbulent fluid flows through energy-consistent stochastic interpolants
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05852v1)
- **Authors**: Nikolaj T. Mücke, Benjamin Sanderse
- **Abstract**: Generative models have demonstrated remarkable success in domains such as text, image, and video synthesis. In this work, we explore the application of generative models to fluid dynamics, specifically for turbulence simulation, where classical numerical solvers are computationally expensive. We propose a novel stochastic generative model based on stochastic interpolants, which enables probabilistic forecasting while incorporating physical constraints such as energy stability and divergence-freeness. Unlike conventional stochastic generative models, which are often agnostic to underlying physical laws, our approach embeds energy consistency by making the parameters of the stochastic interpolant learnable coefficients. We evaluate our method on a benchmark turbulence problem - Kolmogorov flow - demonstrating superior accuracy and stability over state-of-the-art alternatives such as autoregressive conditional diffusion models (ACDMs) and PDE-Refiner. Furthermore, we achieve stable results for significantly longer roll-outs than standard stochastic interpolants. Our results highlight the potential of physics-aware generative models in accelerating and enhancing turbulence simulations while preserving fundamental conservation properties.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

This paper introduces a novel physics-aware generative model for turbulent fluid flow simulation. The method is built upon stochastic interpolants (SIs), which are modified to incorporate energy consistency and divergence-freeness constraints.  Unlike standard stochastic generative models, this approach embeds physical laws by making the SI's parameters learnable coefficients optimized to conserve energy in a statistical sense. The proposed method is evaluated on a benchmark Kolmogorov flow problem and demonstrates improved accuracy, stability, and longer roll-out capabilities compared to existing state-of-the-art alternatives like autoregressive conditional diffusion models (ACDMs) and PDE-Refiner.  The paper emphasizes the potential of physics-aware generative models in accelerating and enhancing turbulence simulations while preserving fundamental conservation properties.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a genuinely novel integration of stochastic interpolants with energy-consistency constraints for turbulence simulation. The optimization of the SI parameters to statistically enforce energy conservation, rather than simply relying on a neural network to learn the physics implicitly, is a significant contribution. While SIs have been explored before for other applications, and machine learning for turbulence is an active area, the *specific combination* of these elements to enforce a physical constraint is new. The hard constraint for divergence-freeness during the SDE solving is also a practical and well-justified addition.
*   **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing a critical challenge in using machine learning for scientific computing: ensuring physical realism and stability. Traditional ML models often lack guarantees of satisfying conservation laws, leading to unstable or physically implausible simulations. This work is a step toward overcoming this limitation by explicitly embedding physical knowledge into the model architecture and training process. Successfully generating more stable and accurate long-term turbulence simulations via machine learning offers the potential to significantly accelerate research and engineering applications in fluid dynamics. The relative ease of inference also provides an advantage over methods that require a fixed number of steps.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Explicit Physics Integration:** Successfully integrates energy consistency and divergence-freeness into the generative model.
    *   **Improved Stability and Accuracy:** Demonstrates improved performance compared to ACDMs and PDE-Refiner on the Kolmogorov flow benchmark, especially concerning long-term stability and energy distribution.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** Clearly explains the proposed approach, including the mathematical formulations and the implementation details.
    *   **Thorough Evaluation:** Provides a comprehensive evaluation using various metrics (MSE, LSiM, energy spectrum, rate of change) and qualitative visualizations.
    *   **Computational benefits:** The paper mentions the benefits of using a single time step versus unrolling over multiple steps. This could enable significant computational advantages.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Benchmark Limitations:** The Kolmogorov flow, while a standard benchmark, is relatively simple compared to more complex turbulent flows encountered in real-world applications. More extensive evaluation on other challenging cases (e.g., channel flow, jet flow) would strengthen the results.
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity:** The performance of the method is likely sensitive to the choice of hyperparameters, neural network architecture, and training procedures. The paper could benefit from a more in-depth discussion of these aspects and potential ways to address them.
    *   **Knowledge of energy evolution:** This method requires the knowledge of an expected average energy change. The authors state the set k_tau = 0 for energy conservation in this case, but this may be difficult to compute in cases with more complex dynamics.
    *   **Computational Cost:** While the paper claims efficiency, it lacks a detailed comparison of the computational cost of training and inference with other methods, particularly regarding the projection step for divergence-freeness.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The framework presented in this work is a crucial step but likely still requires substantial adjustments before being used in many real-world applications of interest.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper could influence future research in physics-informed machine learning for fluid dynamics by:

    *   Encouraging the development of generative models that explicitly incorporate physical constraints.
    *   Inspiring new approaches for optimizing stochastic interpolants and other generative models to enforce conservation laws.
    *   Providing a benchmark for evaluating the performance of physics-aware machine learning models for turbulence simulation.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field by introducing a novel and promising approach for physics-aware generative modeling of turbulent flows. The successful integration of energy consistency and divergence-freeness into stochastic interpolants represents a meaningful advancement over existing methods. The improved stability and accuracy demonstrated on the Kolmogorov flow benchmark suggest the potential for accelerating and enhancing turbulence simulations. However, the limitations of the benchmark, the potential parameter sensitivity, and the lack of detailed computational cost analysis warrant a conservative evaluation. A slightly higher score would be warranted with stronger evidence of robustness on a wider array of test cases and a more complete computational performance analysis. The paper is well-written and presents a clear methodology, making it a valuable contribution that should stimulate further research in this area.

- **Classification**: cs.CE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Enhancing Coreference Resolution with Pretrained Language Models: Bridging the Gap Between Syntax and Semantics
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05855v1)
- **Authors**: Xingzu Liu, Songhang deng, Mingbang Wang, Zhang Dong, Le Dai, Jiyuan Li, Ruilin Nong
- **Abstract**: Large language models have made significant advancements in various natural language processing tasks, including coreference resolution. However, traditional methods often fall short in effectively distinguishing referential relationships due to a lack of integration between syntactic and semantic information. This study introduces an innovative framework aimed at enhancing coreference resolution by utilizing pretrained language models. Our approach combines syntax parsing with semantic role labeling to accurately capture finer distinctions in referential relationships. By employing state-of-the-art pretrained models to gather contextual embeddings and applying an attention mechanism for fine-tuning, we improve the performance of coreference tasks. Experimental results across diverse datasets show that our method surpasses conventional coreference resolution systems, achieving notable accuracy in disambiguating references. This development not only improves coreference resolution outcomes but also positively impacts other natural language processing tasks that depend on precise referential understanding.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework to enhance coreference resolution in pretrained language models (PLMs) by explicitly bridging the gap between syntax and semantics. The approach integrates syntactic parsing and semantic role labeling to capture finer distinctions in referential relationships. It leverages state-of-the-art PLMs to generate contextual embeddings and employs an attention mechanism for fine-tuning coreferential links. Extensive experiments across various datasets demonstrate that the proposed method outperforms traditional coreference resolution systems, particularly in resolving ambiguous references. The authors claim improvements not only in coreference resolution but also in related natural language processing (NLP) tasks that rely on precise referential understanding.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its explicit combination of syntactic and semantic information within a PLM-based coreference resolution framework. While previous research has touched upon either syntax or semantics, the authors argue that their unified approach offers a more comprehensive solution. However, it's crucial to examine the extent to which this combination genuinely leads to *significant* performance gains compared to simpler or existing methods. The claimed improvements need to be substantial enough to justify the added complexity.

*   **Significance:** Coreference resolution is a foundational NLP task, and improvements in this area can benefit various downstream applications. The significance hinges on the practical impact of the reported performance gains and whether the method is generalizable and scalable. The paper highlights improved accuracy in resolving ambiguous references, which is crucial for downstream tasks.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper presents a clear methodology and experimental setup.
    *   It includes ablation studies to evaluate the contribution of individual components of the framework (syntax integration, semantic enhancements, attention mechanism).
    *   It performs experiments on a diverse set of datasets, which enhances the generalizability of the findings.
    *   The integration of syntactic and semantic information is a potentially promising approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Comparison to Recent SOTA:** The paper compares against older methods. The field moves rapidly, and a more rigorous comparison with very recent state-of-the-art methods is necessary to truly demonstrate a significant advance.
    *   **Lack of Deep Theoretical Justification:** While the intuition behind combining syntax and semantics is reasonable, the paper lacks a deeper theoretical analysis of why this specific integration method is superior to other possible approaches. A better understanding of *how* the syntax/semantics integration leads to improved disambiguation would strengthen the work.
    *   **Syntactic Parse Errors:** The reliance on potentially noisy syntactic parses could be a significant limitation. The paper acknowledges this briefly but doesn't adequately address the potential for error propagation. How robust is the system to inaccurate syntactic information?
    *   **Ambiguity in Claims:** Some claims, such as improved performance on "related natural language processing tasks," are vague. More specific examples and evaluations of downstream tasks would be more compelling.
    *  **Reproducibility:** The paper uses several external resources (PLMs, syntax parsers, etc.) making the overall system more complicated and difficult to reproduce. An effort to simplify the resources used would increase the practical value of the method.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the reported performance gains are robust and generalizable, this work could influence future research in coreference resolution by highlighting the importance of joint syntactic and semantic modeling. However, the field is extremely active, and this paper's contribution needs to be placed into proper context with regard to current SOTA models.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, the paper makes a moderate contribution to the field. The integrated approach is interesting, but the lack of a stronger theoretical foundation, potential sensitivity to noisy syntactic parsing, and the need for comparisons against very recent SOTA limit its impact. Further, the reproducibility concerns, and ambiguity around the "improvement to related tasks" also decrease its potential impact.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Agent Guide: A Simple Agent Behavioral Watermarking Framework
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05871v1)
- **Authors**: Kaibo Huang, Zhongliang Yang, Linna Zhou
- **Abstract**: The increasing deployment of intelligent agents in digital ecosystems, such as social media platforms, has raised significant concerns about traceability and accountability, particularly in cybersecurity and digital content protection. Traditional large language model (LLM) watermarking techniques, which rely on token-level manipulations, are ill-suited for agents due to the challenges of behavior tokenization and information loss during behavior-to-action translation. To address these issues, we propose Agent Guide, a novel behavioral watermarking framework that embeds watermarks by guiding the agent's high-level decisions (behavior) through probability biases, while preserving the naturalness of specific executions (action). Our approach decouples agent behavior into two levels, behavior (e.g., choosing to bookmark) and action (e.g., bookmarking with specific tags), and applies watermark-guided biases to the behavior probability distribution. We employ a z-statistic-based statistical analysis to detect the watermark, ensuring reliable extraction over multiple rounds. Experiments in a social media scenario with diverse agent profiles demonstrate that Agent Guide achieves effective watermark detection with a low false positive rate. Our framework provides a practical and robust solution for agent watermarking, with applications in identifying malicious agents and protecting proprietary agent systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Agent Guide, a novel behavioral watermarking framework designed for intelligent agents operating in digital ecosystems like social media. Unlike traditional content watermarking, Agent Guide focuses on embedding watermarks at the *behavioral* level (high-level decisions), rather than the token level of generated text.  It achieves this by biasing the agent's decision-making process (e.g., the probability of choosing to "bookmark" a post) while aiming to preserve the naturalness of the actual action taken (e.g., the specific tags used when bookmarking). The framework uses a z-statistic-based analysis for watermark detection, evaluating detection effectiveness and false positive rates across various simulated agent profiles.  Experimental results in a social media setting show promising watermark detection with low false positives.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:* The paper's primary novelty lies in its focus on *behavioral* watermarking for intelligent agents. Content watermarking has been extensively explored, but the paper correctly identifies its limitations in the context of agents that perform complex, multi-step actions. Shifting the watermarking target to the agent's high-level decision-making processes is a significant departure.  This approach directly addresses the gap in traceability and accountability in agent systems. The decoupling of "behavior" and "action" is a clever way to maintain the agent's natural output while still allowing for watermark embedding.

*Significance:* The significance of the work stems from the increasing deployment of AI agents in sensitive domains where traceability is critical (e.g., detecting disinformation campaigns, protecting intellectual property).  By providing a means to identify and trace agent behavior, Agent Guide has the potential to contribute to safer and more accountable agent systems.  The experimental results, while conducted in a relatively simple social media scenario, demonstrate the feasibility of the approach.  The framework's ability to operate effectively across different agent profiles (active/inactive, various moods) adds to its robustness. The statistical analysis provides a mathematically rigorous framework for detecting the watermarks.

*Strengths:*
*   **Novel approach:**  Shifting the focus to behavioral watermarking is a genuinely novel idea.
*   **Practical application:** The problem being addressed is highly relevant and timely, given the rise of AI agents.
*   **Technical Rigor:** The z-statistic approach provides a statistically sound method for watermark detection.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments, although somewhat limited, provide initial evidence of the framework's effectiveness and robustness.
*   **Clear exposition:** The paper is well-written and explains the concepts clearly.

*Weaknesses:*
*   **Limited Scenarios:** The experimental evaluation focuses on a single social media scenario. While this is a good starting point, the framework's performance in other domains (e.g., financial trading, healthcare) needs to be assessed.
*   **Simplicity of Agent Profiles:**  The agent profiles used in the experiments are relatively basic.  More complex and realistic agent behaviors would provide a more rigorous evaluation.
*   **Action Naturalness Validation:** The paper claims to preserve the naturalness of the agent's "actions," but this is not directly evaluated. User studies or semantic analysis would be needed to support this claim.
*   **Lack of Adversarial Testing:** The paper does not consider potential adversarial attacks on the watermarking scheme (e.g., attempts to remove or evade the watermark). This is a crucial aspect for any practical watermarking system.

*Overall Assessment:*

Agent Guide introduces a promising approach to address the growing need for traceability in intelligent agent systems. While further research is needed to address the limitations discussed above, the concept of behavioral watermarking has significant potential and warrants further investigation. The paper is a valuable contribution to the field, but the limited scope of the evaluation and the lack of adversarial testing prevent it from achieving a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### HybriMoE: Hybrid CPU-GPU Scheduling and Cache Management for Efficient MoE Inference
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05897v1)
- **Authors**: Shuzhang Zhong, Yanfan Sun, Ling Liang, Runsheng Wang, Ru Huang, Meng Li
- **Abstract**: The Mixture of Experts (MoE) architecture has demonstrated significant advantages as it enables to increase the model capacity without a proportional increase in computation. However, the large MoE model size still introduces substantial memory demands, which usually requires expert offloading on resource-constrained platforms and incurs significant overhead. Hybrid CPU-GPU inference has been proposed to leverage CPU computation to reduce expert loading overhead but faces major challenges: on one hand, the expert activation patterns of MoE models are highly unstable, rendering the fixed mapping strategies in existing works inefficient; on the other hand, the hybrid CPU-GPU schedule for MoE is inherently complex due to the diverse expert sizes, structures, uneven workload distribution, etc. To address these challenges, in this paper, we propose HybriMoE, a hybrid CPU-GPU inference framework that improves resource utilization through a novel CPU-GPU scheduling and cache management system. HybriMoE introduces (i) a dynamic intra-layer scheduling strategy to balance workloads across CPU and GPU, (ii) an impact-driven inter-layer prefetching algorithm, and (iii) a score-based caching algorithm to mitigate expert activation instability. We implement HybriMoE on top of the kTransformers framework and evaluate it on three widely used MoE-based LLMs. Experimental results demonstrate that HybriMoE achieves an average speedup of 1.33$\times$ in the prefill stage and 1.70$\times$ in the decode stage compared to state-of-the-art hybrid MoE inference framework. Our code is available at: https://github.com/PKU-SEC-Lab/HybriMoE.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HybriMoE, a hybrid CPU-GPU scheduling and cache management framework designed to improve the efficiency of Mixture of Experts (MoE) inference on resource-constrained devices. The core innovation lies in addressing the challenges posed by the dynamic activation patterns of MoE models and the complexities of hybrid CPU-GPU scheduling.  HybriMoE employs three key techniques: (i) dynamic intra-layer scheduling to balance workloads between CPU and GPU, (ii) impact-driven inter-layer prefetching to anticipate expert needs, and (iii) score-based caching to mitigate the instability of expert activations. The system is implemented on top of the kTransformers framework and evaluated on various MoE-based LLMs, demonstrating significant speedups compared to existing hybrid scheduling methods in both the prefill and decode stages.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a good level of novelty by combining several optimizations specifically tailored for the characteristics of MoE models in a hybrid CPU-GPU environment. While prior work has explored expert offloading and hybrid inference, HybriMoE's combination of dynamic scheduling, impact-driven prefetching, and score-aware caching is a unique and valuable contribution. The focus on adapting to the *dynamic* nature of MoE activation, rather than relying on static or historical data alone, is a significant improvement over existing approaches. The impact-driven prefetching also appears to be a novel approach and seems potentially very effective.

*   **Significance:** Improving the efficiency of MoE inference is highly relevant, as MoE models are crucial for scaling LLMs while maintaining computational feasibility.  The demonstrated speedups (1.33x in prefill and 1.70x in decode) are practically significant and could lower the barrier to deploying large MoE models on edge devices or resource-constrained systems.  The paper's open-source release of the code will further enable other researchers to build upon these ideas and adopt HybriMoE in their systems.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly identifies a critical problem in hybrid MoE inference: adapting to dynamic activation patterns.
    *   The proposed solution is well-reasoned and integrates several complementary techniques.
    *   The experimental evaluation is thorough, covering different models, input lengths, and cache configurations.
    *   The ablation study demonstrates the contribution of each component of HybriMoE.
    *   The code is publicly available, promoting reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The experiments are limited to a single GPU (NVIDIA RTX A6000) and a specific CPU (Intel Xeon Gold 5220R).  While the results are promising, it is unclear how well HybriMoE would generalize to other hardware configurations. More diverse platform evaluation would strengthen the claims.
    *   While the ablation study sheds light on the contribution of different techniques, it's not possible to disentangle the interactions between individual methods fully. More fine-grained ablation studies might reveal insights on potential synergies of specific components.
    *   The simulation phase for scheduling decisions might introduce overhead, especially for larger models and input sizes. The paper doesn't explicitly discuss or analyze the time complexity of this simulation or its impact on overall performance.
    *   The paper mentions Marlin quantization for improved computational efficiency but doesn't provide details on how it's integrated or its effect on model accuracy.

*   **Potential Influence:** HybriMoE has the potential to influence future research on efficient MoE inference, particularly in hybrid CPU-GPU environments. Its dynamic scheduling and impact-driven prefetching techniques could inspire new approaches to resource management and task allocation. The open-source implementation will also facilitate the adoption of HybriMoE in real-world applications.

*   **Justification of Score:** Considering the novelty of combining dynamic scheduling, prefetching, and caching with MoE structure, its significant speedup, thorough evaluation, and open-source code, the paper presents a valuable contribution to the field. However, the limited hardware diversity in the evaluation and the lack of deeper dive on the complexity analysis preclude a higher score.
**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Assessing Thai Dialect Performance in LLMs with Automatic Benchmarks and Human Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05898v1)
- **Authors**: Peerat Limkonchotiwat, Kanruethai Masuk, Surapon Nonesung, Chalermpun Mai-On, Sarana Nutanong, Wuttikorn Ponwitayarat, Potsawee Manakul
- **Abstract**: Large language models show promising results in various NLP tasks. Despite these successes, the robustness and consistency of LLMs in underrepresented languages remain largely unexplored, especially concerning local dialects. Existing benchmarks also focus on main dialects, neglecting LLMs' ability on local dialect texts. In this paper, we introduce a Thai local dialect benchmark covering Northern (Lanna), Northeastern (Isan), and Southern (Dambro) Thai, evaluating LLMs on five NLP tasks: summarization, question answering, translation, conversation, and food-related tasks. Furthermore, we propose a human evaluation guideline and metric for Thai local dialects to assess generation fluency and dialect-specific accuracy. Results show that LLM performance declines significantly in local Thai dialects compared to standard Thai, with only proprietary models like GPT-4o and Gemini2 demonstrating some fluency
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new benchmark for evaluating the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) on Thai local dialects (specifically Isan, Lanna, and Dambro). Recognizing the current bias in existing benchmarks towards Central Thai, the authors created a dataset covering five NLP tasks: summarization, question answering, translation, conversation, and food-related tasks. The paper also presents a novel human evaluation guideline and metric tailored to assess the generation fluency and dialect-specific accuracy of LLMs in these dialects.  Experimental results demonstrate a significant performance decline for LLMs on local Thai dialects compared to Central Thai, with only proprietary models like GPT-4o and Gemini2 showing some fluency. The paper highlights the limitations of traditional evaluation metrics like BLEU and ROUGE-L when assessing dialectal text and underscores the need for more flexible evaluation methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in several aspects. First, the creation of a Thai local dialect benchmark is a valuable contribution, as existing resources are heavily skewed towards the standard language. This specifically addresses a gap in the LLM evaluation landscape. Second, the introduction of a human evaluation guideline and metric designed to assess fluency and dialect-specific accuracy is a significant step beyond traditional metrics that often fail to capture the nuances of dialectal language. The emphasis on phonetic similarity is also a nice touch.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its focus on underrepresented languages and dialects. As LLMs become increasingly prevalent, it is crucial to ensure that they are robust and consistent across different linguistic variations. The paper provides evidence of a significant performance gap, highlighting the need for further research and development in this area. The work pushes the field to consider the nuances of local dialects beyond just measuring word overlap. It offers practical guidance for evaluating LLMs' dialectal competence.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined Problem:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of limited resources for evaluating LLMs on Thai local dialects.
    *   **Comprehensive Benchmark:** The benchmark covers a reasonable range of NLP tasks, providing a holistic assessment of LLM performance.
    *   **Novel Evaluation Metric:** The proposed human evaluation guideline and metric are well-justified and address the limitations of traditional metrics. The Kappa scores provided as evidence of inter-annotator agreement are a nice touch.
    *   **Empirical Evidence:** The experimental results provide strong evidence of the performance decline for LLMs on local Thai dialects.
    *   **Reproducibility:** Releasing both code and the dataset is a big plus.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:**  While the benchmark covers three major dialects, Thailand has more linguistic diversity. Expanding the benchmark to include more dialects would further enhance its value. The limitation of 5 annotators, although yielding a good Kappa score, could be improved with more annotators to increase statistical power of the human evaluation.
    *   **Lack of Analysis of the Models:** The paper mainly focuses on benchmark development and LLM evaluation. A deeper analysis of why certain models (e.g., GPT-4o, Gemini2) perform better on local dialects could be valuable. This might involve examining the training data or architectural differences.
    *   **Limited Comparison with Existing Work:** The paper does a good job of positioning itself with regard to other benchmarks. However, future work should perhaps incorporate or build on related research regarding code switching and related topics.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence the field by:
    *   Motivating further research on developing LLMs that are more robust and consistent across different dialects.
    *   Providing a valuable resource for evaluating LLMs on Thai local dialects.
    *   Serving as a template for developing similar benchmarks for other underrepresented languages and dialects.
    *   Raising awareness of the importance of considering linguistic diversity in LLM development.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:** The paper presents a solid and well-executed study with clear novelty and significance. The creation of the benchmark and the proposed evaluation metric are valuable contributions. The paper is well-written and provides strong empirical evidence. However, the limited scope (three dialects) and the lack of deeper analysis of the models' behavior prevent it from achieving a higher score. Future work should address these limitations and expand the benchmark to include more dialects and analysis. Despite these limitations, the paper offers a significant advancement in the field of LLM evaluation and has the potential to motivate further research in this area.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### PRIMEDrive-CoT: A Precognitive Chain-of-Thought Framework for Uncertainty-Aware Object Interaction in Driving Scene Scenario
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05908v1)
- **Authors**: Sriram Mandalika, Lalitha V, Athira Nambiar
- **Abstract**: Driving scene understanding is a critical real-world problem that involves interpreting and associating various elements of a driving environment, such as vehicles, pedestrians, and traffic signals. Despite advancements in autonomous driving, traditional pipelines rely on deterministic models that fail to capture the probabilistic nature and inherent uncertainty of real-world driving. To address this, we propose PRIMEDrive-CoT, a novel uncertainty-aware model for object interaction and Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning in driving scenarios. In particular, our approach combines LiDAR-based 3D object detection with multi-view RGB references to ensure interpretable and reliable scene understanding. Uncertainty and risk assessment, along with object interactions, are modelled using Bayesian Graph Neural Networks (BGNNs) for probabilistic reasoning under ambiguous conditions. Interpretable decisions are facilitated through CoT reasoning, leveraging object dynamics and contextual cues, while Grad-CAM visualizations highlight attention regions. Extensive evaluations on the DriveCoT dataset demonstrate that PRIMEDrive-CoT outperforms state-of-the-art CoT and risk-aware models.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents PRIMEDrive-CoT, a novel framework designed to improve autonomous driving scene understanding by incorporating uncertainty-aware object interaction and Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning. It aims to address the limitations of traditional autonomous driving pipelines that rely on deterministic models and fail to capture the probabilistic nature of real-world driving scenarios. The approach combines LiDAR-based 3D object detection with multi-view RGB images, utilizes Bayesian Graph Neural Networks (BGNNs) to model uncertainty and object interactions, and leverages CoT reasoning for interpretable decision-making. Grad-CAM visualizations are used to highlight the regions of attention. The framework is evaluated on the DriveCoT dataset, showing improved performance compared to existing CoT and risk-aware models, especially in challenging conditions. The paper argues that by integrating uncertainty estimation and CoT, it moves closer to realizing Agentic AI systems capable of perceiving, reasoning, and acting autonomously in complex environments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addressing a Relevant Problem:** The paper tackles a crucial challenge in autonomous driving: the need for uncertainty-aware reasoning in dynamic and unpredictable environments.
    *   **Novel Integration:** The integration of LiDAR and Multi-view RGB combined with BGNN for uncertainty estimation and CoT reasoning seems to be a relatively novel combination. The framework brings together several existing approaches into a novel combination.
    *   **Uncertainty Aware Reasoning:** The introduction of proximity-aware risk computation enables vehicle to prioritise objects of concern.
    *   **Promising Results:** The reported improvements on the DriveCoT dataset are encouraging, indicating the potential of the proposed approach. The paper quantifies the performance enhancement when using their proposed method
    *   **Explainability:** The incorporation of Grad-CAM and CoT reasoning contributes to explainability, which is essential for safety-critical applications like autonomous driving.
    *   **Completeness:** The authors included an ablation study and a state of the art comparison to reinforce their method and result.
    *   **Agentic:** The work helps move towards Agentic systems that can act autonomously in real-world environments.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dataset Dependency:** The evaluation is limited to the DriveCoT dataset, which is a simulated environment. Real-world validation is needed to assess the framework's robustness and generalizability.
    *   **Computational Cost:** While the inference time is reported, more detailed information on the computational cost of the BGNNs and CoT module would be beneficial, especially for real-time deployment.
    *   **Limited Detail on the CoT Module:** The description of the CoT module could be more detailed. How is the CoT module implemented and trained? Are there specific prompts or templates used to generate the reasoning traces?

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    The core novelty lies in the specific combination of elements: uncertainty-aware BGNNs integrated with CoT reasoning for autonomous driving. While BGNNs and CoT are not entirely novel concepts on their own, their application and integration in this specific context is. The paper convincingly demonstrates how such an integration can achieve better performance than existing state-of-the-art systems.

    The significance of the work comes from addressing the key challenges of reliability and safety in autonomous systems by incorporating uncertainty and explainability. This potentially moves the field a step closer to real-world deployment of these systems.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a worthwhile contribution to the field of autonomous driving by addressing a crucial problem with a novel and well-executed approach. The results are promising, and the integration of uncertainty and explainability is commendable. However, the validation on a simulated dataset limits the impact. Real-world testing is required to improve the reliability of the results.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper provides a compelling combination of methods to address a critical issue in autonomous driving: a method to estimate uncertainty within autonomous driving decisions. The combination of methods is innovative, the paper is well written and explains the system well. However, the paper lacks thoroughness of real-world testing. The authors included an ablation study that significantly increases the strength of the paper. For these reasons, I believe that this is a promising and novel direction and deserves a 7.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### CKGAN: Training Generative Adversarial Networks Using Characteristic Kernel Integral Probability Metrics
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05945v1)
- **Authors**: Kuntian Zhang, Simin Yu, Yaoshu Wang, Makoto Onizuka, Chuan Xiao
- **Abstract**: In this paper, we propose CKGAN, a novel generative adversarial network (GAN) variant based on an integral probability metrics framework with characteristic kernel (CKIPM). CKIPM, as a distance between two probability distributions, is designed to optimize the lowerbound of the maximum mean discrepancy (MMD) in a reproducing kernel Hilbert space, and thus can be used to train GANs. CKGAN mitigates the notorious problem of mode collapse by mapping the generated images back to random noise. To save the effort of selecting the kernel function manually, we propose a soft selection method to automatically learn a characteristic kernel function. The experimental evaluation conducted on a set of synthetic and real image benchmarks (MNIST, CelebA, etc.) demonstrates that CKGAN generally outperforms other MMD-based GANs. The results also show that at the cost of moderately more training time, the automatically selected kernel function delivers very close performance to the best of manually fine-tuned one on real image benchmarks and is able to improve the performances of other MMD-based GANs.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the paper and can provide a summary and critical evaluation.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CKGAN, a novel Generative Adversarial Network (GAN) variant built upon an integral probability metrics framework utilizing characteristic kernels (CKIPM). CKIPM is designed as a distance metric between probability distributions, optimizing a lower bound of the Maximum Mean Discrepancy (MMD) within a reproducing kernel Hilbert space. This approach is intended to address the mode collapse problem in GANs by mapping generated images back to random noise.  The paper also proposes a "soft selection" method for automatically learning a characteristic kernel function, avoiding manual kernel selection.  Experiments on synthetic and real image datasets (MNIST, CelebA, LSUN) are presented, demonstrating that CKGAN often outperforms other MMD-based GANs.  The results also suggest the automatically selected kernel performs comparably to manually fine-tuned kernels, sometimes improving performance over other MMD-based GANs, at the cost of more training time.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper introduces two main novelties:

1.  **CKIPM as a Lower Bound for MMD:** Using a characteristic kernel to define an IPM that serves as a *lower bound* to the standard MMD is a valuable theoretical contribution. The standard MMD is often difficult to optimize directly.

2.  **Soft Kernel Selection:** The automatic selection of a characteristic kernel by a linear combination of several kernels during training is a practical contribution aimed at simplifying the application of MMD-based GANs and improving robustness. The *simplicity* of the method is a strength. Instead of learning the parameters of kernel functions directly (e.g., bandwidth of Gaussian kernels) the paper proposes to linearly combine characteristic kernels, where the weights are learnable.

**Significance/Impact:**

*   **Addressing Mode Collapse:** Addressing mode collapse is a significant goal in GAN research. By mapping generated data back to the noise space, the authors attempt to stabilize training and promote diversity. Although this idea is not entirely new, it is presented within a new framework.
*   **Practical Applicability:**  The soft kernel selection strategy has practical implications. It removes a significant barrier to entry for using MMD-based GANs, as manually tuning kernels can be tedious and requires expert knowledge.  The experimental results suggest that the automated selection can achieve comparable performance to manual tuning and improve performance compared to other MMD-based GANs.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The paper includes empirical evaluations on several standard datasets and compares against established baselines, lending credibility to the proposed approach.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and presents the key concepts clearly.
*   **Theoretical Grounding:** The paper provides a reasonable level of theoretical justification for CKIPM.
