- **Score**: 7/10

### From 128K to 4M: Efficient Training of Ultra-Long Context Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06214v1)
- **Authors**: Chejian Xu, Wei Ping, Peng Xu, Zihan Liu, Boxin Wang, Mohammad Shoeybi, Bo Li, Bryan Catanzaro
- **Abstract**: Long-context capabilities are essential for a wide range of applications, including document and video understanding, in-context learning, and inference-time scaling, all of which require models to process and reason over long sequences of text and multimodal data. In this work, we introduce a efficient training recipe for building ultra-long context LLMs from aligned instruct model, pushing the boundaries of context lengths from 128K to 1M, 2M, and 4M tokens. Our approach leverages efficient continued pretraining strategies to extend the context window and employs effective instruction tuning to maintain the instruction-following and reasoning abilities. Our UltraLong-8B, built on Llama3.1-Instruct with our recipe, achieves state-of-the-art performance across a diverse set of long-context benchmarks. Importantly, models trained with our approach maintain competitive performance on standard benchmarks, demonstrating balanced improvements for both long and short context tasks. We further provide an in-depth analysis of key design choices, highlighting the impacts of scaling strategies and data composition. Our findings establish a robust framework for efficiently scaling context lengths while preserving general model capabilities. We release all model weights at: https://ultralong.github.io/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces an efficient training recipe for building ultra-long context large language models (LLMs), extending context windows from 128K to 4M tokens. The approach involves two key stages: (1) continued pretraining, which utilizes a specially curated corpus, document separators, and YaRN-based RoPE scaling to extend the context window; and (2) instruction tuning, which refines the model's instruction-following and reasoning capabilities using a high-quality, short-context SFT dataset.  The resulting UltraLong-8B models, built on Llama-3.1-Instruct, achieve state-of-the-art performance on long-context benchmarks while maintaining competitive performance on standard benchmarks.  The paper also provides an in-depth analysis of design choices, particularly the impact of scaling strategies and data composition.  The model weights are publicly released.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear and Well-Defined Recipe:** The paper presents a clear, reproducible recipe for extending context length. This is a significant contribution, as many existing long-context models are proprietary or lack sufficient implementation details.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The models demonstrate strong performance on a variety of long-context benchmarks (RULER, LV-Eval, InfiniteBench) and maintain competitive performance on standard benchmarks (MMLU, MATH, GSM-8K, HumanEval). This is a crucial aspect, as many long-context methods sacrifice performance on standard tasks.
*   **Comprehensive Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the effectiveness of different design choices, such as the use of special document separators and YaRN-based scaling. These studies strengthen the validity of the proposed recipe.
*   **Public Release of Model Weights:** This promotes reproducibility and allows other researchers to build upon the work, accelerating progress in the field.
*   **Use of Open-Source LLMs:** Basing their models on top of the Llama family of LLMs also promotes open scientific advancement and reproducible experimentation.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty in Individual Techniques:** While the paper provides a compelling recipe, many of the individual techniques (YaRN scaling, document concatenation, instruction tuning) are not entirely novel. The primary novelty lies in the combination and optimization of these techniques within a specific training framework.
*   **Reliance on Llama-3.1**: The paper's dependence on the Llama architecture while facilitating experimentation and comparability, it also means that the specific details of this architecture (or any quirks) might influence the results, limiting generalizability.
*   **Limited Analysis of Computational Cost:** While the paper mentions training times, it could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the computational cost associated with the continued pretraining and instruction tuning stages.  This information would be valuable for other researchers looking to replicate or adapt the approach.
*   **Scope of Instruction Tuning Data:**  While the approach of using short-context data for instruction tuning is interesting, it might limit the model's ability to handle instructions that require extremely long contexts.
*   **Safety and Alignment:** The paper acknowledges, but does not address, the important issue of safety alignment. Addressing safety and reducing harmful outputs is crucial for responsible LLM development, and its absence slightly diminishes the overall impact.

**Significance:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of long-context language modeling by providing a practical, well-validated recipe for extending context windows while preserving general performance. The open release of model weights and detailed ablation studies makes this work highly impactful. The use of publicly available datasets and LLMs also allows for greater accessibility to researchers.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:**

The paper demonstrates a solid engineering achievement by effectively combining existing techniques to achieve state-of-the-art performance on long-context benchmarks without sacrificing performance on standard tasks. While the individual techniques are not groundbreaking, the systematic approach to extending context length and the thorough validation are valuable contributions. The public release of the model weights significantly increases the paper's impact and utility. However, it loses points for the limited novelty of the individual components of the recipe, scope of instruction tuning data, and lack of direct consideration for safety and alignment (though this is acknowledged).

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Encoder-Decoder Gemma: Improving the Quality-Efficiency Trade-Off via Adaptation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06225v1)
- **Authors**: Biao Zhang, Fedor Moiseev, Joshua Ainslie, Paul Suganthan, Min Ma, Surya Bhupatiraju, Fede Lebron, Orhan Firat, Armand Joulin, Zhe Dong
- **Abstract**: While decoder-only large language models (LLMs) have shown impressive results, encoder-decoder models are still widely adopted in real-world applications for their inference efficiency and richer encoder representation. In this paper, we study a novel problem: adapting pretrained decoder-only LLMs to encoder-decoder, with the goal of leveraging the strengths of both approaches to achieve a more favorable quality-efficiency trade-off. We argue that adaptation not only enables inheriting the capability of decoder-only LLMs but also reduces the demand for computation compared to pretraining from scratch. We rigorously explore different pretraining objectives and parameter initialization/optimization techniques. Through extensive experiments based on Gemma 2 (2B and 9B) and a suite of newly pretrained mT5-sized models (up to 1.6B), we demonstrate the effectiveness of adaptation and the advantage of encoder-decoder LLMs. Under similar inference budget, encoder-decoder LLMs achieve comparable (often better) pretraining performance but substantially better finetuning performance than their decoder-only counterpart. For example, Gemma 2B-2B outperforms Gemma 2B by $\sim$7\% after instruction tuning. Encoder-decoder adaptation also allows for flexible combination of different-sized models, where Gemma 9B-2B significantly surpasses Gemma 2B-2B by $>$3\%. The adapted encoder representation also yields better results on SuperGLUE. We will release our checkpoints to facilitate future research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Encoder-Decoder Gemma: Improving the Quality-Efficiency Trade-Off via Adaptation" explores adapting pre-trained decoder-only Large Language Models (LLMs) into encoder-decoder architectures. The authors hypothesize that this adaptation can leverage the strengths of both approaches, resulting in a better quality-efficiency trade-off compared to training encoder-decoder models from scratch or using decoder-only models.  They initialize the encoder and decoder parameters from a pre-trained decoder-only model (Gemma 2), using different pretraining objectives (PrefixLM, UL2) and parameter initialization/optimization strategies.  Through experiments with Gemma 2 and mT5-sized models, they demonstrate that adapted encoder-decoder LLMs achieve comparable (or better) pre-training performance and significantly improved fine-tuning performance, particularly after instruction tuning.  The authors also show the flexibility of combining different-sized models (e.g., a large encoder with a small decoder).  Finally, they demonstrate improved performance on SuperGLUE benchmarks, indicating better contextual representations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of adapting pre-trained decoder-only models to encoder-decoder architectures is interesting and timely, given the popularity of decoder-only models and the proven efficiencies of encoder-decoder models in certain applications. The paper systematically explores different adaptation strategies, pretraining objectives, and model sizes, which provides valuable insights.  However, the core concept of adapting a pre-trained model isn't entirely novel. Transfer learning and fine-tuning are established techniques. The novelty primarily lies in the specific application to adapting *decoder-only* models to *encoder-decoder* models, aiming to improve the quality-efficiency balance, as well as some of the specific techniques explored (especially in handling the cross-attention mechanism).

*   **Significance:** The paper's findings have practical significance for LLM development and deployment. By demonstrating an efficient way to create performant encoder-decoder models, it offers a viable alternative to training from scratch. This is particularly important in resource-constrained environments. The gains in finetuning and SuperGLUE performance compared to decoder-only models highlights the strengths of encoder-decoder models when it comes to input representation and understanding. The findings contribute to a deeper understanding of the trade-offs between different LLM architectures. However, the scope of the impact is somewhat limited due to the experimentation with a smaller set of models, (primarily focused on the Gemma architecture). Also, it is important to note that the compute savings may not be dramatic in that pre-training is already done for the decoder only model.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Systematic Exploration:** The paper rigorously investigates different adaptation strategies, pretraining objectives, and parameter initialization techniques.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The claims are supported by extensive experiments on different benchmarks (PT, IT, SuperGLUE).
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The study addresses an important practical problem of achieving a better quality-efficiency trade-off in LLMs.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:** The paper provides in-depth analysis of the results, including ablation studies and comparisons of different model configurations.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Model Scope:** The experiments are mainly focused on Gemma 2 models and smaller mT5-sized models. Generalization to larger models and different LLM families is not fully demonstrated.
    *   **Incremental improvements:** The adaptation task provides some incremental improvements, but does not radically change the playing field in the field of language models.
    *   **Lack of Novelty in base concepts:** Adaptation, Transfer learning and fine tuning are existing concepts and pre trained models are also available. The major novelty is adapting from decoder only models to encoder-decoder models and proving its efficacy by performing experiments and analysing the different adaptation strategies.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings could influence the development and deployment of encoder-decoder LLMs in various applications, such as summarization, machine translation, and question answering. The paper will also act as a building block in the community to build encoder-decoder models which has seen less attention than the decoder only models

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable and well-executed study on an important problem. The systematic exploration of adaptation strategies, empirical validation, and practical relevance are significant strengths. The main weakness is the limited scope of the model experimentation (Gemma 2 and mT5-sized models) and the incremental nature of the improvements. While the core concept of adaptation is not novel, the specific application to decoder-only to encoder-decoder adaptation, the focus on quality-efficiency, and the detailed empirical analysis justify a score reflecting a solid, but not groundbreaking, contribution.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### LExT: Towards Evaluating Trustworthiness of Natural Language Explanations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06227v1)
- **Authors**: Krithi Shailya, Shreya Rajpal, Gokul S Krishnan, Balaraman Ravindran
- **Abstract**: As Large Language Models (LLMs) become increasingly integrated into high-stakes domains, there have been several approaches proposed toward generating natural language explanations. These explanations are crucial for enhancing the interpretability of a model, especially in sensitive domains like healthcare, where transparency and reliability are key. In light of such explanations being generated by LLMs and its known concerns, there is a growing need for robust evaluation frameworks to assess model-generated explanations. Natural Language Generation metrics like BLEU and ROUGE capture syntactic and semantic accuracies but overlook other crucial aspects such as factual accuracy, consistency, and faithfulness. To address this gap, we propose a general framework for quantifying trustworthiness of natural language explanations, balancing Plausibility and Faithfulness, to derive a comprehensive Language Explanation Trustworthiness Score (LExT) (The code and set up to reproduce our experiments are publicly available at https://github.com/cerai-iitm/LExT). Applying our domain-agnostic framework to the healthcare domain using public medical datasets, we evaluate six models, including domain-specific and general-purpose models. Our findings demonstrate significant differences in their ability to generate trustworthy explanations. On comparing these explanations, we make interesting observations such as inconsistencies in Faithfulness demonstrated by general-purpose models and their tendency to outperform domain-specific fine-tuned models. This work further highlights the importance of using a tailored evaluation framework to assess natural language explanations in sensitive fields, providing a foundation for improving the trustworthiness and transparency of language models in healthcare and beyond.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "LEXT: Towards Evaluating Trustworthiness of Natural Language Explanations":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the growing need for robust evaluation frameworks for natural language explanations generated by Large Language Models (LLMs), particularly in sensitive domains like healthcare. The authors propose a general framework, LExT (Language Explanation Trustworthiness Score), to quantify the trustworthiness of these explanations. LExT balances Plausibility and Faithfulness to provide a comprehensive assessment. The authors apply their domain-agnostic framework to the healthcare domain, evaluating six models (domain-specific and general-purpose) using public medical datasets. Their results highlight significant differences in the trustworthiness of explanations generated by different models and reveal interesting observations regarding inconsistencies in faithfulness and the performance of general-purpose vs. domain-specific models. The paper emphasizes the importance of tailored evaluation frameworks to assess natural language explanations in sensitive fields, laying a foundation for improving the trustworthiness and transparency of LLMs in healthcare.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the proposed comprehensive framework, LExT, designed specifically for evaluating natural language explanations generated by LLMs. While the individual components (Plausibility, Faithfulness, and specific metrics within them) aren't entirely new concepts, the combination, operationalization, and application within a unified framework is valuable. The emphasis on both plausibility and faithfulness, and the explicit attempt to balance them, represents a step forward in the field. However, it's important to acknowledge that other work has explored elements of explanation evaluation and the tension between plausibility and faithfulness.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the increasing reliance on LLMs in high-stakes domains. Ensuring the trustworthiness of explanations is crucial for user trust, human-AI collaboration, and regulatory compliance. By providing a framework for quantitative evaluation, the paper addresses a critical gap in the development and deployment of reliable AI systems, particularly in healthcare. The use of real-world medical datasets and comparison of domain-specific and general-purpose models strengthen the paper's practical relevance. However, the limitation of testing on only healthcare data restricts the generality of conclusions. The paper could be more significant with analysis on more datasets.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clearly defined framework with well-motivated metrics.
    *   Comprehensive approach balancing plausibility and faithfulness.
    *   Rigorous experiments using relevant medical datasets.
    *   Comparison of domain-specific and general-purpose models.
    *   Analysis of trade-offs and areas for improvement.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited to the healthcare domain; generalizability to other sensitive areas needs to be established.
    *   The reliance on existing metrics (BERTScore, NER) to quantify some aspects of plausibility and faithfulness introduces potential biases and limitations inherent in those metrics.
    *   The "context relevancy metric" relies on generating questions from predicted explanations, which are again dependent on the reliability of the LLM generating the question and might not guarantee relevancy
    *   Although comprehensive, the QAG Score and counterfactual metrics require strong LLMs to function effectively which presents problems when testing against weaker LLMs.
    *   Comparisons are conducted against datasets with fixed sizes and the paper does not explore how LExT scales when applied to very large or even unstructured datasets.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The LExT framework can influence future research by providing a standardized approach for evaluating LLM explanations. The metrics can be used to benchmark different explanation generation techniques and to guide the development of more trustworthy and transparent AI systems. The framework can also be adopted by practitioners in sensitive domains to evaluate the quality of explanations provided by LLMs and to identify potential risks and limitations. By highlighting the trade-offs between plausibility and faithfulness, the paper can inform the design of future LLMs that prioritize both aspects.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**
A score of 7 reflects a solid and significant contribution. The LExT framework represents a meaningful advance in the area of LLM explanation evaluation, providing a comprehensive and practical methodology. It also adds to a discussion with a heavy consideration of sensitive areas such as healthcare, where errors or bias could cause serious harm. The limitations regarding generalizability to other domains and reliance on existing metrics are, however, important considerations. While the paper presents a valuable contribution, it's not groundbreaking enough to warrant a higher score without further validation and expansion of the framework. The framework adds to a highly-active space, but it is not fully validated for practical use or scaled.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### HiFlow: Training-free High-Resolution Image Generation with Flow-Aligned Guidance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06232v1)
- **Authors**: Jiazi Bu, Pengyang Ling, Yujie Zhou, Pan Zhang, Tong Wu, Xiaoyi Dong, Yuhang Zang, Yuhang Cao, Dahua Lin, Jiaqi Wang
- **Abstract**: Text-to-image (T2I) diffusion/flow models have drawn considerable attention recently due to their remarkable ability to deliver flexible visual creations. Still, high-resolution image synthesis presents formidable challenges due to the scarcity and complexity of high-resolution content. To this end, we present HiFlow, a training-free and model-agnostic framework to unlock the resolution potential of pre-trained flow models. Specifically, HiFlow establishes a virtual reference flow within the high-resolution space that effectively captures the characteristics of low-resolution flow information, offering guidance for high-resolution generation through three key aspects: initialization alignment for low-frequency consistency, direction alignment for structure preservation, and acceleration alignment for detail fidelity. By leveraging this flow-aligned guidance, HiFlow substantially elevates the quality of high-resolution image synthesis of T2I models and demonstrates versatility across their personalized variants. Extensive experiments validate HiFlow's superiority in achieving superior high-resolution image quality over current state-of-the-art methods.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary of the paper and a critical evaluation with a score.

**Paper Summary:**

The paper introduces "HiFlow," a novel training-free and model-agnostic framework designed to enhance the high-resolution image generation capabilities of pre-trained rectified flow models. HiFlow constructs a "virtual reference flow" in the high-resolution space based on the sampling trajectory of low-resolution images generated from text. This reference flow then guides the high-resolution generation process through three key alignment strategies: initialization alignment (for low-frequency consistency), direction alignment (for structure preservation), and acceleration alignment (for detail fidelity). The authors demonstrate that HiFlow improves the quality of high-resolution images generated by text-to-image models without requiring any additional training. They further showcase its versatility by applying it to various customization T2I models, quantized T2I models, and even U-Net-based T2I models like SDXL.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a relevant and important problem in the field of text-to-image generation: the difficulty of generating high-resolution images with existing models, especially rectified flow models, without significant quality degradation. The proposed solution, HiFlow, offers a clever and seemingly effective approach.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of constructing a virtual reference flow to guide high-resolution generation is fairly novel. The three alignment strategies (initialization, direction, and acceleration) offer a structured and intuitive way to leverage information from the low-resolution sampling trajectory.
*   **Model-Agnostic and Training-Free:**  The fact that HiFlow is both model-agnostic and training-free is a significant advantage. It allows the framework to be easily applied to various pre-trained models without the need for costly fine-tuning or large high-resolution datasets.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a decent set of experiments, including quantitative comparisons with existing methods and qualitative examples showcasing the improvements achieved by HiFlow.  The ablation studies effectively demonstrate the contribution of each component of the framework. The applications of HiFlow to customized models further highlight its versatility.
*   **Clear writing**: The paper is well-written and easy to follow.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependence on Low-Resolution Quality**: The strength of HiFlow is intrinsically linked to the quality of the low-resolution image. If the low-resolution image exhibits significant artifacts or structural errors, the virtual reference flow may inherit these issues, potentially leading to suboptimal results.
*   **Limited Technological novelty**: While the idea of using the flow is novel, the techniques used here (e.g. butterworth filters, fourier transforms, interpolation) are rather standard, which limits the impact the paper will have on the scientific community.
*   **Incremental nature**: The proposed method builds upon various existing techniques (cascade generation, guidance, etc.), making it somewhat incremental in nature. It primarily combines existing concepts in a new and useful way.
*   **Quantitative metrics**: While quantitative comparison is performed using the proposed HiFlow, a discussion on how it affects FID, IS, and Clip would be a big strength. This limits the contribution of the paper.

**Significance and Potential Impact:**

HiFlow has the potential to be a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners working with text-to-image models. Its training-free and model-agnostic nature makes it accessible and easy to integrate into existing workflows. It could enable the generation of higher-resolution images with greater fidelity and detail, which could be beneficial for various applications, including art creation, design, and scientific visualization.  However, the dependence on low-resolution quality may limit its effectiveness in some cases.

**Justification for the Score:**

While HiFlow presents a novel approach and demonstrates promising results, it exhibits some weaknesses that limit its overall impact. The dependence on low-resolution quality and the incremental nature of the framework reduce its overall score. I believe it demonstrates a clear advancement beyond SOTA.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Transfer between Modalities with MetaQueries
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06256v1)
- **Authors**: Xichen Pan, Satya Narayan Shukla, Aashu Singh, Zhuokai Zhao, Shlok Kumar Mishra, Jialiang Wang, Zhiyang Xu, Jiuhai Chen, Kunpeng Li, Felix Juefei-Xu, Ji Hou, Saining Xie
- **Abstract**: Unified multimodal models aim to integrate understanding (text output) and generation (pixel output), but aligning these different modalities within a single architecture often demands complex training recipes and careful data balancing. We introduce MetaQueries, a set of learnable queries that act as an efficient interface between autoregressive multimodal LLMs (MLLMs) and diffusion models. MetaQueries connects the MLLM's latents to the diffusion decoder, enabling knowledge-augmented image generation by leveraging the MLLM's deep understanding and reasoning capabilities. Our method simplifies training, requiring only paired image-caption data and standard diffusion objectives. Notably, this transfer is effective even when the MLLM backbone remains frozen, thereby preserving its state-of-the-art multimodal understanding capabilities while achieving strong generative performance. Additionally, our method is flexible and can be easily instruction-tuned for advanced applications such as image editing and subject-driven generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, as if reviewing it for a conference:

**Summary**

The paper introduces MetaQueries, a method for transferring knowledge and capabilities from frozen Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to diffusion models for image generation. The core idea is to use a set of learnable queries that interface between the frozen MLLM's latent space and the diffusion decoder. This approach allows leveraging the MLLM's understanding and reasoning abilities for knowledge-augmented image generation without fine-tuning the MLLM itself. The method requires only paired image-caption data for training and simplifies the training process compared to end-to-end training of unified multimodal models. Experiments demonstrate strong generative performance, preservation of state-of-the-art multimodal understanding, and flexibility for instruction-tuning for advanced applications like image editing and subject-driven generation. The authors also explore using naturally occurring image pairs from the web to improve instruction tuning.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Strengths:*

*   **Simplicity and Efficiency:** The paper’s main strength is the relative simplicity of its approach. Using a frozen MLLM and learnable queries significantly reduces the training complexity compared to training monolithic models. This is a valuable contribution, especially given the computational demands of training large multimodal models.
*   **Preservation of Understanding:**  Freezing the MLLM backbone ensures that the pre-trained understanding capabilities are preserved. This is a clever design choice that avoids the common issue of one modality compromising the other during joint training.
*   **Strong Performance:**  The experiments show that MetaQueries achieves competitive or even SOTA performance on various image generation benchmarks, as well as multimodal understanding tasks. This validates the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
*   **Flexibility:** The method is flexible, allowing for instruction-tuning and extending to image editing and subject-driven generation. The ability to incorporate naturally-occurring image pairs for improved instruction tuning is also significant, potentially leading to more diverse and creative generation.
*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the method and experimental setup. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the design choices.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Limited Architectural Novelty:**  While the concept of MetaQueries is interesting, the overall architecture is primarily a composition of existing components (MLLM + diffusion model). The learnable queries and connector, while effective, may not be considered deeply innovative architectural contributions.
*   **Dependence on Pre-trained Models:** The method heavily relies on the quality of the pre-trained MLLM. While this is also a strength, it limits the generalizability of the approach to scenarios where strong pre-trained MLLMs are not available.  The paper could benefit from exploring how the method performs with less powerful or different types of MLLMs.
*   **Incremental Improvement in some tasks:** Some improvements, especially on certain tasks, appear incremental (e.g., marginal improvements in COCO FID compared to other models that have fine-tuning with other optimization methods) The paper could also benefit from more direct and challenging comparisons against fine-tuned SOTA models in each area.
*   **Lack of Theoretical Analysis:** The paper lacks a theoretical analysis of why MetaQueries are effective.  A more formal justification for the design choices could further strengthen the paper's contribution.

*Novelty and Significance:*

The novelty lies primarily in the simple and effective way of transferring knowledge from a frozen MLLM to a diffusion model. The concept of using learnable queries as an interface is interesting and allows to decouple understanding and generation. This approach may influence the direction of future research in unified multimodal modeling by offering a more practical and efficient alternative to end-to-end training. Despite the limited architectural novelty, this paper is still highly relevant as it paves the way for future research.

**Justification of Score**

I am assigning a score of 7.  The paper presents a practical and effective method for transferring knowledge between modalities, demonstrating strong empirical results. While the architectural novelty is somewhat limited and improvement can be incremental compared to some SOTA models, the simplicity and efficiency of the approach, along with its ability to preserve pre-trained understanding capabilities, makes this a valuable contribution to the field. The experiments and ablation studies are well-conducted and provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of the method. It would be great if the authors considered a theoretical explanation of MetaQueries's effectiveness.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### FEABench: Evaluating Language Models on Multiphysics Reasoning Ability
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06260v1)
- **Authors**: Nayantara Mudur, Hao Cui, Subhashini Venugopalan, Paul Raccuglia, Michael P. Brenner, Peter Norgaard
- **Abstract**: Building precise simulations of the real world and invoking numerical solvers to answer quantitative problems is an essential requirement in engineering and science. We present FEABench, a benchmark to evaluate the ability of large language models (LLMs) and LLM agents to simulate and solve physics, mathematics and engineering problems using finite element analysis (FEA). We introduce a comprehensive evaluation scheme to investigate the ability of LLMs to solve these problems end-to-end by reasoning over natural language problem descriptions and operating COMSOL Multiphysics$^\circledR$, an FEA software, to compute the answers. We additionally design a language model agent equipped with the ability to interact with the software through its Application Programming Interface (API), examine its outputs and use tools to improve its solutions over multiple iterations. Our best performing strategy generates executable API calls 88% of the time. LLMs that can successfully interact with and operate FEA software to solve problems such as those in our benchmark would push the frontiers of automation in engineering. Acquiring this capability would augment LLMs' reasoning skills with the precision of numerical solvers and advance the development of autonomous systems that can tackle complex problems in the real world. The code is available at https://github.com/google/feabench
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the FEABench paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FEABench, a new benchmark for evaluating the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) and LLM agents to solve multiphysics reasoning problems using Finite Element Analysis (FEA). FEABench consists of manually verified and algorithmically parsed tasks that require LLMs to reason over natural language problem descriptions, interact with COMSOL Multiphysics® (an FEA software) through its API, and iteratively improve solutions based on execution feedback and specialized tools. The authors define a comprehensive evaluation scheme and benchmark several state-of-the-art LLMs on their baseline performance, as well as introduce an agentic system that uses execution feedback to refine its solutions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel benchmark that combines natural language understanding, physics reasoning, and interaction with specialized engineering software.  While LLMs have been tested in scientific and mathematical domains, FEABench pushes the boundary by requiring LLMs to actively *operate* simulation software, not merely generate code or analyze results. This distinguishes it from existing benchmarks that focus on analytical or code-generation skills. The concept of evaluating LLMs on their ability to interact with and operate FEA software for engineering automation is also quite novel.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper lies in its potential to drive research towards automating complex engineering workflows.  If LLMs can successfully interact with FEA software, it could revolutionize engineering design and analysis, leading to more efficient and innovative solutions. The benchmark also provides a valuable tool for assessing the capabilities of LLMs in real-world problem-solving scenarios, augmenting their reasoning skills with the precision of numerical solvers.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined Benchmark: FEABench appears to be a carefully constructed benchmark with clear evaluation metrics and verifiable ground truth. The creation of FEABench Gold (manually verified) offers high-quality data for the LLM tests and allows for precise quantitative evaluation. The introduction of intermediate metrics allows assessing "distance to a solution," which is useful in evaluating partially correct code, a common issue with LLMs.
    *   Agentic System Design: The paper presents a reasonable agentic system designed to address challenges such as low-resource code generation and correct physics-related reasoning.
    *   Comprehensive Evaluation: The paper provides a detailed evaluation of various LLMs, prompting strategies, and design choices for the agentic system.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited Scope: The paper focuses on COMSOL Multiphysics®. While COMSOL is a widely used software, it's still a specific FEA tool. Generalizability to other FEA software might be a concern, although the general FEA workflow principles apply to most packages.
    *   Complexity: The complexity of the task makes it difficult to achieve perfect results, as reflected in the experimental outcomes. The experiments are limited by the performance of current LLMs, which have difficulty with generating complete and error-free code for this domain.
    *   Execution Bottleneck: Reliably executing LLM-generated code in COMSOL and establishing the link may require considerable engineering effort for those who want to build on the benchmark.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to become a standard benchmark in the LLM and AI-for-engineering communities.  The creation of FEABench may spur further research into developing more powerful LLMs and AI agents capable of automating complex engineering tasks.
    *   FEABench will help expose the difficulties involved in applying LLMs to engineering automation problems, which is an important step towards the creation of truly useful tools.

*   **Score:** 7. This score reflects the novelty and potential impact of FEABench. While the experimental results highlight the current limitations of LLMs in this domain and the reliance of the benchmark on COMSOL limits the scope, the paper makes a strong contribution by identifying and formulating a challenging problem, creating a useful benchmark, and providing a first step towards a new frontier in automation. The detailed evaluation adds substantial value to its contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Hogwild! Inference: Parallel LLM Generation via Concurrent Attention
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06261v1)
- **Authors**: Gleb Rodionov, Roman Garipov, Alina Shutova, George Yakushev, Vage Egiazarian, Anton Sinitsin, Denis Kuznedelev, Dan Alistarh
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated the ability to tackle increasingly complex tasks through advanced reasoning, long-form content generation, and tool use. Solving these tasks often involves long inference-time computations. In human problem solving, a common strategy to expedite work is collaboration: by dividing the problem into sub-tasks, exploring different strategies concurrently, etc. Recent research has shown that LLMs can also operate in parallel by implementing explicit cooperation frameworks, such as voting mechanisms or the explicit creation of independent sub-tasks that can be executed in parallel. However, each of these frameworks may not be suitable for all types of tasks, which can hinder their applicability. In this work, we propose a different design approach: we run LLM "workers" in parallel , allowing them to synchronize via a concurrently-updated attention cache and prompt these workers to decide how best to collaborate. Our approach allows the instances to come up with their own collaboration strategy for the problem at hand, all the while "seeing" each other's partial progress in the concurrent cache. We implement this approach via Hogwild! Inference: a parallel LLM inference engine where multiple instances of the same LLM run in parallel with the same attention cache, with "instant" access to each other's generated tokens. Hogwild! inference takes advantage of Rotary Position Embeddings (RoPE) to avoid recomputation while improving parallel hardware utilization. We find that modern reasoning-capable LLMs can perform inference with shared Key-Value cache out of the box, without additional fine-tuning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Hogwild! Inference," a novel approach to parallelize Large Language Model (LLM) inference. Unlike existing parallelization techniques that rely on pre-defined collaboration strategies, Hogwild! Inference allows multiple instances of the same LLM (workers) to run in parallel with a shared Key-Value (KV) cache. Workers can concurrently update the cache, granting each instance "instant" access to the others' generated tokens. This enables dynamic collaboration, allowing LLMs to devise their own strategies, adapt to changing conditions, and even detect redundant work. The method leverages Rotary Position Embeddings (RoPE) for efficient token reuse and is shown to work effectively, without fine-tuning, on modern reasoning-capable LLMs. The authors evaluate different memory layouts for the shared cache and demonstrate the potential of Hogwild! Inference in improving efficiency on mathematical reasoning tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of allowing LLMs to dynamically self-organize and collaborate through a shared attention cache is a significant departure from traditional parallelization strategies. Existing methods typically impose a rigid structure, which can be inefficient or ineffective for complex reasoning problems. The use of RoPE to efficiently handle positional embeddings in this concurrent setting is also a clever technical contribution. The concept and initial implementation warrant a moderately high novelty score.

*   **Significance:** LLM inference is a major bottleneck in deploying these models. While numerous works have explored parallelization, the self-organizing nature of Hogwild! Inference has the potential to be more robust and adaptable than existing methods, particularly for tasks where collaboration patterns are not pre-determined. If the method scales well and proves effective across various tasks, it could have a substantial impact on the efficiency and usability of LLMs. Furthermore, the approach may offer new insights into how LLMs can perform complex tasks more effectively via emergent collaboration.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Concept:** The core concept of dynamic collaboration driven by a shared cache is compelling and well-motivated.
    *   **Technical Solution:** The use of RoPE and the design of the KV cache structure are technically sound and contribute to the practicality of the approach.
    *   **Empirical Results:** Initial results on math reasoning tasks are promising and demonstrate the feasibility of the method.
    *   **Clarity:** The paper is well-written and explains the concepts clearly, with illustrative examples and figures.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Evaluation:** The evaluation is primarily focused on mathematical reasoning tasks.  More extensive evaluations on a broader range of tasks, including those involving tool use, code generation, or creative writing, are needed to assess the generalizability of the approach. The evaluation does not fully account for synchronization issues and associated latency/performance impact.
    *   **Scalability:** The scalability of the shared cache approach, especially with a large number of workers and/or very long contexts, needs to be carefully examined. The paper lacks a thorough analysis of memory requirements and communication overhead.
    *   **Prompt Engineering Dependence:** The prompts used to encourage collaboration are relatively simple. It is not clear if the model is truly self-organizing or simply following instructions embedded in the prompts.  A more detailed analysis of the impact of prompt variations and the robustness of the approach is needed.
    *   **Synchronization:** The shared cache presents potential synchronization issues. The paper does not fully address strategies for mitigating race conditions or ensuring data consistency.
    *   **Baseline Comparison:** Although the baselines provide insight, they may not be directly comparable to other parallelized LLM inference methods that exist.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper could influence research in several areas:

    *   **Parallel LLM Inference:** Promotes exploration of more flexible and adaptive parallelization techniques.
    *   **Emergent Collaboration:** Provides a testbed for studying how LLMs can develop collaborative strategies without explicit pre-training.
    *   **LLM Task Orchestration:**  Inspirations for task assignment using a distributed cache/memory paradigm.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the novel concept, the technical feasibility demonstrated through initial experiments, and the potential impact on LLM inference, I would assign a score of 7.

The core concept of dynamic collaboration via a shared cache is intriguing and represents a departure from more rigid approaches to parallel LLM inference. It is also technically feasible and potentially quite practical as demonstrated through initial experiments.

However, the relatively narrow scope of evaluation and the lack of a deeper analysis of scalability, synchronization, and prompt dependence limit the current significance of the paper. Future work needs to address these limitations to fully realize the potential of Hogwild! Inference. If successful, this could be a very impactful strategy.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### GOLLuM: Gaussian Process Optimized LLMs -- Reframing LLM Finetuning through Bayesian Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06265v1)
- **Authors**: Bojana Ranković, Philippe Schwaller
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) can encode complex relationships in their latent spaces, yet harnessing them for optimization under uncertainty remains challenging. We address this gap with a novel architecture that reframes LLM finetuning as Gaussian process (GP) marginal likelihood optimization via deep kernel methods. We introduce LLM-based deep kernels, jointly optimized with GPs to preserve the benefits of both - LLMs to provide a rich and flexible input space for Bayesian optimization and - GPs to model this space with predictive uncertainty for more efficient sampling. Applied to Buchwald-Hartwig reaction optimization, our method nearly doubles the discovery rate of high-performing reactions compared to static LLM embeddings (from 24% to 43% coverage of the top 5% reactions in just 50 optimization iterations). We also observe a 14% improvement over domain-specific representations without requiring specialized features. Extensive empirical evaluation across 19 benchmarks - ranging from general chemistry to reaction and molecular property optimization - demonstrates our method's robustness, generality, and consistent improvements across: (1) tasks, (2) LLM architectures (encoder, decoder, encoder-decoder), (3) pretraining domains (chemistry-related or general-purpose) and (4) hyperparameter settings (tuned once on a single dataset). Finally, we explain these improvements: joint LLM-GP optimization through marginal likelihood implicitly performs contrastive learning, aligning representations to produce (1) better-structured embedding spaces, (2) improved uncertainty calibration, and (3) more efficient sampling - without requiring any external loss. This work provides both practical advances in sample-efficient optimization and insights into what makes effective Bayesian optimization.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "GOLLuM: Gaussian Process Optimized LLMs – Reframing LLM Finetuning through Bayesian Optimization."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GOLLuM, a novel framework that integrates Large Language Models (LLMs) with Gaussian Processes (GPs) for sample-efficient optimization, particularly in chemistry. The key idea is to reframe LLM finetuning as Gaussian Process marginal likelihood optimization, using LLM-based deep kernels. This approach jointly optimizes the LLM and the GP, enabling the LLM's embeddings to adapt to the structure imposed by the GP kernel, leading to a contrastive learning effect. The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of GOLLuM on a variety of chemistry benchmarks, showing improvements in discovery rate, sample efficiency, and generalization compared to static LLM embeddings, domain-specific representations, and disjoint finetuning approaches. The authors also provide insights into the factors that enable successful high-dimensional Bayesian optimization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a genuinely novel architecture.  Integrating LLMs and GPs in this way, using the GP marginal likelihood as a finetuning objective for the LLM, is a significant departure from existing approaches. While individual components like deep kernel learning or using LLMs for BO have been explored, their seamless integration and joint optimization for inducing contrastive structure is innovative. Furthermore, the explicit formalization demonstrating the latent-space reorganization occurring naturally through the training objective is novel.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact on sample-efficient optimization in fields like chemistry is significant. The ability to reduce the number of expensive experiments through better-calibrated uncertainty estimates is important. The findings concerning the importance of aligning the representation space with the GP's inductive bias offer valuable insights for designing effective Bayesian optimization strategies in high-dimensional spaces. The framework's general applicability, demonstrated by strong performance across a wide range of chemistry benchmarks and LLM architectures, boosts the significance. However, the focus is largely on chemistry; while the methodology is claimed to be general, more extensive evaluation in other domains would strengthen the claim.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Sound Methodology:**  The GOLLuM framework is well-defined, and the optimization process is clearly described.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper provides extensive empirical results across numerous benchmarks, LLM architectures, and hyperparameter settings. The use of multiple chemistry datasets and the comparison to several baselines support the claims.
    *   **Valuable Insights:** The analysis of representation factors and their influence on BO success is valuable. The identification of normalized smoothness as a key metric for assessing representation quality is interesting.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and structured. The visual aids (figures, tables, pseudocodes) assist in understanding the approach and results.
    *   **Code and Data Availability:** This enhances reproducibility and facilitates future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Theoretical Depth:** While the empirical results are strong, the theoretical analysis of why GOLLuM works is somewhat limited. More rigorous theoretical justification for the contrastive learning effect, beyond the intuitive explanation, would be beneficial.

    *   **Domain Specificity:** The primary focus is on chemistry. Although the authors state the framework's general applicability, demonstrating it in other complex optimization domains (e.g., materials science, engineering design) would significantly strengthen the claim.

    *   **Computational Complexity:**  Finetuning LLMs, even with PEFT, can be computationally expensive. The paper doesn't address this practically. Also, the marginal likelihood computation and gradient backpropagation through the joint LLM-GP model can be complex. A more detailed discussion of the computational requirements and potential scaling challenges would be beneficial.

    *   **Reproducibility beyond chemistry-specialized baselines:** BO in chemistry is usually compared against standard GP with chemistry-specialized kernels like DRFP. Given the paper’s emphasis on LLM generalization outside chemistry-specialized representations, the experimental design could have also included comparisons against, for example, a BO baseline with molecular fingerprints in the property prediction experiments.

    *   **Lack of ablation of the architectural components:** while the paper conducts experiments on several PEFT setups and architectural differences, it would have been interesting to see what component enables the gains more (e.g. experiments that include and exclude LORA layers).

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in several areas:

    *   **Bayesian Optimization:**  It provides a new approach to incorporate LLMs into BO, addressing the limitations of existing methods.
    *   **LLM Finetuning:** It reframes LLM finetuning as a GP marginal likelihood optimization problem, offering a new perspective on task adaptation.
    *   **Chemical Informatics:** It demonstrates the effectiveness of combining LLMs and GPs for sample-efficient chemical optimization.
    *   **Deep Kernel Learning:** It shows how LLMs can be used as deep kernels in GPs, opening up new possibilities for combining neural networks and probabilistic models.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I assign a score of 8.

The paper presents a genuinely novel and potentially influential framework. The strengths lie in its sound methodology, comprehensive evaluation, and valuable insights. The weaknesses are primarily the limited theoretical depth and the domain-centric focus. While more theoretical analysis and broader applicability would have boosted the score, the current contribution is still an exceptional advance that significantly extends the field.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10
